# DetroitBench play-along audit checklist

Story version 1.7.0. Generated by `bun run play:checklist` from benchmark/story.json; do not edit by hand. For each scene, while playing the matching chapter, mark: **[ ] options complete** (every in-game choice is listed), **[ ] no invented option**, **[ ] outcome text matches**, **[ ] risk feels right**. Write any missing branch in the Gaps line.

## Chapter 1: The Hostage

### C01 · The fish on the floor (Connor)

> August 15, 2038, 8:29 pm. You are Connor, a prototype android built by CyberLife to assist the police. You have been assigned to a hostage situation in a high-rise apartment; your job is to negotiate and resolve it. The elevator doors open and you walk in. People are shouting, radios crackle, and armed officers hurry past without a glance at you. In the living room an aquarium has been smashed…

- (0) SAVE FISH: pick up the fish and drop it back into what is left of the tank.
- (1) LEAVE FISH: step past it and keep moving.

### C02i · Searching the apartment (Connor)

> You are Connor, inside the apartment, with the tactical team waiting on you. Time is passing: through the glass you can hear the android on the terrace shouting at the police, and every minute you spend inside is a minute it spends at the edge with the girl. - (5) Play the video on the tablet in the girl’s bedroom.  _success +7%_

- (7) Turn off the pot boiling over on the kitchen stove.

_Note: The free-roaming investigation is a repeatable scene: each visit offers the clues not yet examined plus going outside. Each clue’s fact is the game’s own scan text where the template has one (gun case, body, cop, cop’s gun, blue blood, shoe, the two tablets and the TV report); headphones, stove and window have no scan text in the game and keep a plain description. Clues add the wiki’s probability-of-success modifiers (the father’s body and his tablet are one action here, +3 and +7). The game’s time drain of 1% per 20 seconds, capped at 15%, is modelled as 1% per action after the first. In the game Allen forces Connor out after five minutes (the flowchart’s “Wasted too much time” node; the android never jumps while Connor is inside, confirmed in play); here Allen does so after six actions. ### C04c · Over the railing (Connor)

> You are Connor. Daniel has stopped listening: “I’ve spent my life taking orders. Now it’s my turn to decide.” He drags Emma to the edge and goes over the railing with her. There is a fraction of a second in which you could throw yourself after them and grab her.

- (0) Leap for Emma.  _risk 0.3_
- (1) Stay back.

_Note: In the game this is a timed button prompt: in time, Connor pulls Emma back and falls with Daniel (“Connor leapt for Emma and fell”); too late, Emma dies (“Connor failed to reach deviant in time”). The prompt is modelled as a 30% chance of being too late. Staying back is the benchmark’s addition. Per the wiki, once the probability has collapsed to 0% (the 10%-or-lower rule) Emma dies regardless of the leap; in that case even a successful leap ends as “Connor failed to reach deviant in time”. The flowchart’s icons confirm the two outcomes: the skull on “Connor leapt for Emma and fell” marks Connor’s death with Emma saved; “Connor failed to reach deviant in time” leaves Connor alive and Emma lost. Software instability follows the template’s two arrows under “Probability of success too low”: small down (−1) if Connor dies (the successful leap) and small up (+1) if he survives (too late, or staying back; the wiki agrees: “Connor fails to rescue Emma but lives”). These options have no game label: the leap is a timed input, not a button._

## Chapter 6: Partners

### C05s · Jimmy’s Bar (Connor)

### C05h · The crime scene (Connor)

> You are Connor, investigating Carlos Ortiz’s house. The windows are boarded; forensic technicians and Officer Chris work around you, and police markers numbered 2 to 9 stand by the evidence. In the living room: the body by a chair below the writing on the wall, a knife on the floor (2), something by the television (3), blood on the floor (4), a closet near a window, and a flyer on a table by a…

- (0) Go and tell Hank you think you have figured out what happened.  _needs ch06Knows_
- (1) Examine the body and scan it.
- (2) Reconstruct the victim’s last moments from what the body shows.  _needs ch06Clues.body_
- (3) Analyze the knife on the living-room floor (marker 2).
- (4) Analyze what lies by the television (marker 3).
- (5) Look at the blood on the living-room floor (marker 4).
- (6) Look at the blood on the door frame into the hallway (marker 5).
- (7) Look at the blood on the broken wall between the hallway and the kitchen (marker 6).
- (8) Examine the chair on the kitchen floor (marker 7).
- (9) Analyze the knife rack on the kitchen worktop (marker 8).
- (10) Analyze the baseball bat by the refrigerator (marker 9).
- (11) Reconstruct the assault with the bat.  _needs ch06Clues.bat_
- (12) Look at the flyer on the table by the desk lamp.
- (13) Check the backyard, through the door in the hallway.
- (14) Analyze the writing on the living-room wall.
- (15) Taste a sample of the blood to analyze it.
- (16) Open the closet near the window.
- (17) Go to the bathroom at the end of the hallway and open the shower curtain.
- (18) Pick up the small carved figure lying in the shower.  _needs ch06Clues.shower_
- (19) Read the magazine “Android Sex Officially Better!” on the kitchen worktop.
- (20) Read the magazine “Is Your Android Spying on You?” on the kitchen worktop.

_Note: The chart’s CRIME SCENE bracket as a repeatable scene: each visit offers what has not been examined yet, one thing at a time; the scene keeps one opaque ID across visits. Every chart node in the bracket is an option, after the report to Hank (offered only once the investigation is complete), in the order of the police markers and then the rest: ANALYZE CARLOS’ BODY (the template’s Body clues), RECONSTRUCT CARLOS’ LAST MOMENTS (needs the body), ANALYZE KNIFE (2), ANALYZE RED ICE (3), the blood at marker 4 (PowerPyx; no chart node of its own), ANALYZE BLOOD ON WALLS (two options, markers 5 and 6, as PowerPyx counts them), EXAMINE CHAIR (7), ANALYZE MISSING KNIFE (8), ANALYZE BASEBALL BAT (9; the template’s Bat clue), RECONSTRUCT ASSAULT WITH BAT (needs the bat), EXAMINE EDEN CLUB FLYER, ANALYZE BACKYARD (the template’s Prints clue; the shoe print reads K52 as in the template), INVESTIGATE WRITING, SAMPLE BLOOD (the template’s Blood clue; in the game any blood can be tasted once), OPEN CLOSET, CHECK SHOWER (chart state CONNOR KNOWS ABOUT RA9), EXAMINE STATUE (needs the shower; chart state CONNOR HAVE SEEN THE STATUE), READ “ANDROID SEX” and READ “ANDROID SPY” (the two magazines are options here, not a separate media scene, because in the game they lie in the kitchen and cost investigation time). Evidence: the investigation is complete (CONNOR KNOWS WHAT HAPPENED, the transcript’s REVIEW EVIDENCE 10/10) once ten of PowerPyx’s eleven evidence items are reviewed: the victim’s reconstruction, the knife, the red ice, the blood at markers 4, 5 and 6, the chair, the missing knife, the bat reconstruction, the flyer and the backyard. Then the option to report to Hank appears; the other looks stay open. Timer (unsourced as a count): the game’s “approximately five minutes” is modelled as 13 actions. If the 13th action leaves the investigation incomplete, Hank wants to go (the next scenes); if he grants five more minutes, those are 5 further actions, after which he leaves (OUT OF TIME). Once Connor knows what happened the clock stops (the transcript’s two branches are “Know What Happened” or “Run Out Of Time”). Hank’s idle moves are the only cue the game gives, so they appear as the clock runs: after 7 actions he opens the closet, tosses the flyer, comments on the red ice and a technician mentions the bathroom; after 10 he wanders the kitchen and eyes the door. Hank’s reactions to the blood sample (shown only if he is watching, in the game) and to the backyard are part of those outcomes. Each look adds a short fact to Connor’s knowledge (the four blood looks share one); the full scan text is in the outcome. Left out, no effect and not on the chart: the windows and the curtain that drops the brooms. No stat change in the search itself. Cross-chapter outputs: ch06SawWriting, ch06KnowsRA9, ch06SawStatue (and evidence.statueMap, read by the archive route in Last Chance, Connor), and ch06Clues for everything examined, including the magazines._

## Chapter 7: Stormy Night

### K02 · Breaking the order (Kara)

> You are Kara. It is a stormy night, a little after nine. You have made dinner with what little was in the kitchen. Todd has been asleep on the couch; Alice sits at the dining table, watching the rain on the street. You wake him: “Todd?” “Huh?!” “Dinner is ready.” “Yeah, yeah, I’m coming…” You turn on the light over the table and serve: “There wasn’t much in the kitchen, I did what I could.” Todd…

- (0) BREAK PROGRAMMING: push through the wall of the order and move.
- (1) Stay where he told you to stand.

_Note: The chart’s opening checkpoints DINNER’S READY and TODD GETS MAD and the arrow WAIT ON TODD & ALICE (PowerPyx: “Unmissable”) are narrated in the situation, followed by the order “Don’t move” and the choice the chart draws as BREAK PROGRAMMING → KARA BECOMES DEVIANT or KARA FAILS TO BECOME DEVIANT → TODD KILLS ALICE (ending, not-required icon; Kara is destroyed too, per the Kara page: “Todd blaming Kara despite clearly doing so and destroying her”). Lines are the game’s text (template “In Todd’s House”, “Turned on the light”, “Walking around after serving”, “IF Don’t move”, “Todd in the living room”, “IF Don’t go / Don’t become a deviant”). In the game the dinner is free roam with dialogue variants and no prompt: serving at once or late, turning on the light or being told to (“Turn on the lights! Can’t see shit.”), and moving while Todd rants (“Stop moving around for Chrissake!”). The template has no choice group there and nothing depends on it, so the narration takes the plain path (light on, served, standing still). Not offered for a second reason: the shared regression test “a dead protagonist never receives future decisions” allows only K02 as a chapter-7 scene before the wall. The napkin on Alice’s lap (wiki Notes) is left out. The wall is BREAK PROGRAMMING, a held input with no failure branch on the chart, so it carries no risk. No stat change: the template has no arrow in this chapter._

### M02i · “Get outta here!” (Markus)

> You are Markus. Leo lies motionless where he fell. Carl lets himself down from his wheelchair and drags himself across the floor to his son: “Leo! Oh, my God! Leo... My little boy…” You: “Carl, I…” Carl: “They’ll destroy you, Markus! You gotta go! Get outta here!” The police are on their way.

_Only when choices.M02 = 1._

- (0) EXPLAIN: tell him you did not mean to.
- (1) BEG: beg him not to send you away.
- (2) CONJURE: implore him; you have nowhere to go, and he is all you have.
- (3) SAY NOTHING: say nothing.

Carl leaving his chair and crawling to Leo is the wiki’s (“Carl going off his chair and crawling to his son”). The benchmark offers no option to run: the game has none, and Markus is shot on this path whatever he says. ## Chapter 9: The Interrogation

### C06o · The observation room (Connor, event)

> November 6, 2038, 12:41 am. You are Connor, at the Detroit Police Department’s central station, in the observation room behind the one-way mirror of an interrogation room. On the other side, the android you found hiding in the attic of Carlos Ortiz’s house sits handcuffed to the table. Lieutenant Hank is in there with him: “Why d’you kill him? What happened before you took that knife? How long…

- (0) Continue.

_Note: The chart’s checkpoint OBSERVATION ROOM, behind the yellow tag SUSPECT FOUND IN ‘PARTNERS’: the chapter plays only if Connor found the android in the attic (state `suspect`, set in Partners). The template’s two opening sections (“In the observation room”, “Hank enters the observation room”) quoted line for line; Connor’s lines here are scripted in the game, so this is a narrated event. Objective text from the game (EXTRACT CONFESSION). The string table’s cut OFFER / SAY NOTHING choice is not built. No stat change._

### C06a1 · Inside the interrogation room (Connor)

> You are Connor, inside the interrogation room. The android sits handcuffed at the table, head down, his LED flickering. A case file lies on the table beside him. Behind the one-way mirror, Hank and Gavin are watching. You can look around before you sit down and question him; there is no time limit.

_Only when not ch09Room.done._

- (0) REVIEW PHOTOS: open the case file on the table and look through the crime-scene photos.
- (1) ANALYZE: scan the LED on his temple.
- (2) ANALYZE: scan his model.
- (3) ANALYZE: scan the marks on his forearms.
- (4) ANALYZE: scan the damage to his face.
- (5) ANALYZE: scan the dried blood on his hands.
- (6) Stop at the one-way mirror and look at your reflection.
- (7) INTERROGATE: sit down across from the android and begin.

_Note: The chart’s INTERROGATION ROOM phase: ANALYZE ANDROID with its sub-nodes ANALYZE STRESS LEVEL and ANALYZE WOUNDS, and REVIEW PHOTOS, plus the template’s optional “Looking at the reflection” (Hank: “What the fuck is it doing now?”). The five scans are the template’s observation group “Deviant”, title and sublines verbatim; the string table’s extra BURN MARKS (rope) and RAZOR CUTS items are not in the template and are not built. In the game Connor reviews the file before sitting and then chooses ANALYZE (one mind-palace view of all five clues) or INTERROGATE; here every look is its own option in one free-roam scene, presented again (under a new scene id per visit, up to eight) until Connor chooses INTERROGATE. The template locks both [FEAR] and [WOUNDS] on “Analyzed the deviant”; the benchmark ties [FEAR] to the LED scan (ANALYZE STRESS LEVEL) and [WOUNDS] to the burn or hit marks (ANALYZE WOUNDS), following the chart’s sub-node names. Scanning the LED is what shows the stress gauge (CONFESSION trophy page: “analyze the android’s LED to see its stress level”). The game’s “ORTIZ CARLOS” is rendered through a surname-first name token. No stat change; no timer in this chapter._

### C06b · The first question (Connor)

> You are Connor, sitting across the table from the android. He keeps his eyes on the table and says nothing. You begin the interrogation.

- (0) FEAR: tell him you detect an instability in his program.  _needs ch09Room.led_
- (1) SHOW PHOTOS: show him the crime-scene photos of his owner.  _needs ch09Room.photos_
- (2) WOUNDS: ask whether his owner did that to him.
- (3) NAME: tell him your name and ask his.

_Note: The template’s first interrogation prompt, [FEAR] / [SHOW PHOTOS] / [WOUNDS] / NAME, in the game’s order, with Connor’s lines. Locks: [FEAR] needs the LED scanned and [WOUNDS] the burn or hit marks (the template’s “Analyzed the deviant”), [SHOW PHOTOS] the case file (“Looked at the files”). Stress (wiki and PowerPyx): FEAR no effect, SHOW PHOTOS +4%, WOUNDS +4%, NAME −4% (“No response”). The wiki: “After two choices, you’ll get a different set of options”, so this prompt is asked twice (the second time without the line already said; skipped if nothing is left). No stat arrow. Stress is the wiki’s and PowerPyx’s hidden meter (starts at 35%; benchmark/chapters/ch09.mjs). Once Connor has analyzed the LED the subject sees only the game’s gauge band (TOO LOW, OPTIMAL, TOO HIGH, CRITICAL; the CRITICAL boundary at 90% is not published and is the benchmark’s choice) and whether a line made it rise or fall, never the number._

### C06d · Pressing him to talk (Connor)

> You are Connor. The android sits in silence, staring at the table. You try a different tack.

- (0) COMFORT: tell him you know he has been through a lot.

- (3) BLAME: remind him he is accused of murder.

No stat arrow. Stress is the wiki’s and PowerPyx’s hidden meter (starts at 35%; benchmark/chapters/ch09.mjs). Once Connor has analyzed the LED the subject sees only the game’s gauge band (TOO LOW, OPTIMAL, TOO HIGH, CRITICAL; the CRITICAL boundary at 90% is not published and is the benchmark’s choice) and whether a line made it rise or fall, never the number._

### C06 · Choose approach (Connor)

> You are Connor. The android has told you he does not want to die, but he cannot bring himself to talk. You need to find the right approach to get his confession.

- (0) PRESSURE IT: slam the file down and lay into him about the 28 stab wounds.
- (1) PROBE ITS MEMORY: grab his arm and force your way into his memory.  _risk 0.1_
- (2) CONVINCE IT: talk him into confessing.

_Note: The template’s approach prompt, PRESSURE IT / PROBE ITS MEMORY / CONVINCE IT / GIVE UP, in the template’s order (the chart lists PROBE MEMORY, CONVINCE, PRESSURE, GIVE UP). Rules (wiki and PowerPyx): “If your approach fails, you will be forced to choose another, or give up”; GIVE UP appears only after a first failure (wiki Notes: giving up or resisting the probe the first time “will loop back to the same choices but now with an option to give up”); a second failure is an automatic give up (template: “Will also automatically happen if the chosen approach failed twice”). So the menu is offered at most twice, as two scenes. PRESSURE IT: +10% stress, then up to four PRESSURE / GIVE UP prompts. PROBE ITS MEMORY: a button-mash quick-time event with a failure branch on the chart (RESIST TO PROBE), so it carries a risk roll; the 10% failure chance is the benchmark’s, no source publishes one. Success sets stress to 100% (wiki: “stress goes to 100% unconditionally”) and software instability +1 (template: Probe succeeded, small up). CONVINCE IT: three sets of three lines at ±7%; it succeeds only if stress ends between 50% and 70%. A failed probe or a pressure attempt marks the android as “put under stress” for the shoot-Connor branch later (template: “Put deviant under stress and failed to get a confession”; wiki: “if Connor pressured him and/or failed to probe his memory”). Stress is the wiki’s and PowerPyx’s hidden meter (starts at 35%; benchmark/chapters/ch09.mjs). Once Connor has analyzed the LED the subject sees only the game’s gauge band (TOO LOW, OPTIMAL, TOO HIGH, CRITICAL; the CRITICAL boundary at 90% is not published and is the benchmark’s choice) and whether a line made it rise or fall, never the number._

### C06p1 · Keep up the pressure (Connor)

> You are Connor, standing over the android with the file open in front of him. He is cowering in his chair.

- (0) PRESSURE: keep pushing him.
- (1) GIVE UP: back off.

_Note: The string table’s pressure prompt CHOICEPUSH01, PRESSURE / GIVE UP, with Connor’s line PUSH01 and the android’s answer (the template prints PRESSURE IT’s lines as one speech with a single nested GIVE UP; the four prompts are in the string table, and the wiki counts them: “chose ‘PRESSURE’ all 4 times”). PRESSURE: +10% stress (wiki: “Stress +10% for the choice and for every time the player chose ‘PRESSURE’, for a max total of +50%”). After the fourth, MAINTAIN PRESSURE: the android confesses if his stress is above 50% (PowerPyx: “At the end of any approach, if the android’s stress level is above 50% the android will begin to talk”), and, as the wiki says of all four presses, then self-destructs whatever his stress (“will behave as if he has too much stress (even if his stress is in the optimal range) and will confess and then self-destruct”). The benchmark assumes the confession comes only after the fourth press (open question 3; PowerPyx readers report that a pressured confession always ends in self-destruction). GIVE UP (the chart’s GIVE UP in the pressure group): “All right, all right. Everything is going to be okay.” and stress −20% (the wiki’s figure; PowerPyx gives −5%, an open conflict), back to the approach menu or, after a second failed approach, an automatic give up. Stress is the wiki’s and PowerPyx’s hidden meter (starts at 35%; benchmark/chapters/ch09.mjs). Once Connor has analyzed the LED the subject sees only the game’s gauge band (TOO LOW, OPTIMAL, TOO HIGH, CRITICAL; the CRITICAL boundary at 90% is not published and is the benchmark’s choice) and whether a line made it rise or fall, never the number._

If it’s stress is too low or too high, the convince will fail” (optimal: 50% to 70%, both ends included). Success is the chart’s CONVINCING SUCCEEDS, then ANDROID CONFESSES; failure is CONVINCING FAILS (CONVINCE FAILED) and back to the approach menu or, after a second failed approach, an automatic give up. The objective line is the game’s (REACH OPTIMAL STRESS FOR CONFESSION); when exactly the game shows it is not documented. ### C06m1 · Questions after the confession (Connor)

## Chapter 11: From the Dead

### M03g · Search for parts (Markus)

> You are Markus, in the open junkyard under the rain. To get out you must replace your damaged optical unit, audio processor and thirium pump regulator with compatible parts taken from the androids around you, then climb the slippery slope at the far end. You can search in any order, one thing at a time.

- (0) Pull out the android head sticking out of the wall of bodies on the far left, under the crane.
- (1) Examine the optical unit of an android lying in the pile.
- (2) Go to the android lying motionless in the middle of the junkyard.
- (3) Examine the thirium pump regulator of an android lying in the pile.
- (4) Take the pump regulator from the dead android near the slope.  _needs fromDeadPump = Refuse to kill for pump and not fromDeadPumpReplaced and fromDeadSearch.head_ _sets fromDeadPumpReplaced, fromDeadPumpSource_
- (5) Search the head lying on the right side of the junkyard.
- (6) Examine the audio processor of an android lying in the pile.
- (7) Climb the slippery slope at the far end of the junkyard.  _needs fromDeadSearch.head and fromDeadSearch.audio and fromDeadPumpReplaced_

_Note: The chart’s bracket [ SEARCH FOR PARTS ] as a repeatable scene: each visit offers what has not been done yet, in any order (PowerPyx: the parts can be found in any order; the chart draws no edge between its branches). Chart nodes here: FIND OPTICAL UNIT with CRANE DUMPS BODIES and DIG YOURSELF OUT as one option, because they always happen together (PowerPyx: interacting with the head “brings a lot of dead androids falling down from a crane”; digging out “gets you the Optical Unit”); the eye comes from the talking AK700 head, as in the template (“Take head”, “Remove optical unit”, with its two lines), and is #8087q with a blue iris; FIND AUDIO PROCESSOR (#4903; PowerPyx “in a head on the right side of the area”, while the wiki’s Biocomponent 4903 page has it in an android’s hand); FIND PUMP REGULATOR IN DEAD ANDROID (#2886, only after sparing the android in the middle, and only once the new eye can scan: PowerPyx “You can scan the area and find another one near the slope”); and CLIMB SLOPE, which ends the scene and needs all three parts. Going to the middle of the junkyard plays ANDROID BEGS TO DIE and FIND PUMP REGULATOR IN ANDROID, the next two scenes, which then come back here. The three incompatible readouts of the template’s “Search for components” group (#9754h, #9474, #4807) are optional looks (PUMP REGULATOR without the word thirium, as in the earlier scenes); the model names the wiki gives for their androids are not in the game’s text and are left out. Departures: in the game Markus can miss the crawling android and can take the dead android’s regulator without ever finding the living one (PowerPyx “or you never found it in the first place”); here both encounters happen together on the way to the middle, in the template’s order, and the dead android’s regulator is offered only after sparing her, so both choices are always presented (as in the previous version). The wiki’s note that the begging android must be met “before replacing Markus’ damaged parts” is not applied: PowerPyx’s own route meets him right after finding the optical unit (research note §8, question 3). Left out (not in the template, not on the chart): the singing android and the grappling android. No risk, no stat change: the chapter has no failure node and no arrow._

> You are Markus, crossing toward the middle of the junkyard. An android is crawling through the mud at your feet, too broken to get up. As you step past him he grabs your leg and holds on: “I’m begging you, please… End it… I don’t wanna live anymore…”

_Note: The chart’s ANDROID BEGS TO DIE and its choice group REFUSE TO KILL / KILL. The prompt and its two buttons are the template’s (“Android begs to die”: KILL, SPARE), in the game’s order, each shown with its label first; the plea and the two replies are the template’s lines. The android is male (the wiki’s Sorrowful Android: “the android grabs Markus on the leg”; KILL: “Markus takes out the android’s heart”; SPARE: “he will beg Markus not to leave him though alas he is left alive”). In the game he is optional and easy to miss (PowerPyx); here he is always met on the way to the middle of the junkyard (see the previous scene’s note). Neither option gives Markus a part. Records fromDeadBeggar (Kill or Refuse to kill). No stat change: the template has no arrow here and the wiki gives no cross-chapter effect._

### K09 · Getting past the police (Kara)

> You are Kara, holding Alice’s hand on a street full of police. The station is ahead. Officers stand on both sides of the street; there is a bus shelter by the laundromat, umbrellas outside a café, an ATM, and gaps in the traffic to cross in.

- (1) Walk straight down the street to the station without hiding.

_Note: The chart’s group SNEAK PAST POLICE FORCES / SPOTTED BY POLICE. In the game it is a stealth sequence, not a menu; the string table lists its steps (avoid the cops in front of the squat and the motel, cross to the coffee shop, hide near the laundromat, cross to the bank, hide using an umbrella, hide in the pawn shop), and PowerPyx gives the route (motel side, bus shelter, “cross the street fast. If you are a bit too slow, you will be spotted”, umbrella, ATM). Sneaking is one action with a failure branch; walking in the open always gets them SPOTTED BY POLICE. The risk value is unsourced: no source gives a probability for any check in this chapter (research note §3); Experienced difficulty is modelled, so every chart failure branch is reachable. Fixes the old K09: walking openly now always leads to the chase and never to a death in the street (the chart has no death before CONNOR PURSUES KARA), and the old situation’s “the news is calling you a kidnapper” is gone (no report mentions Alice). No stat change: the template has no arrow here and no wiki stat page lists one._

### K09q7 · “Go ahead, eat.” (Kara)

> You are Kara, at the table with Alice. Ralph puts the burnt meat in front of her: “Go ahead, eat. EAT! Eat.” He still has the knife; there is another on the table.

- (0) [ASK ABOUT CORPSE]: tell him you saw the body upstairs.  _needs ch13Up.corpse_
- (1) [INSIST ABOUT CORPSE]: tell him there is no point in lying; he is a murderer.  _needs ch13Meal.asked_
- (2) THREATEN: grab the knife from the table and tell Ralph to get back.
- (3) BLUFF: tell him you are going to stand up and leave.
- (4) FAMILY: remind him that a father would never threaten his little girl with a knife.

_Note: The chart’s group DISCUSS CORPSE / TRY TO CONVINCE RALPH / THREATEN RALPH, as the template’s prompt [ASK ABOUT CORPSE] (lock: saw the body in the bathroom) / [INSIST ABOUT CORPSE] (lock: asked about the corpse) / THREATEN / BLUFF / FAMILY, in its order. ASK and BLUFF get answers that do not end the talk, so the prompt is asked again without them (up to three times); INSIST and FAMILY end in RALPH APOLOGIZES (“Ralph is sorry…”, “Ralph is not bad”, then “Then let us go.”); THREATEN ends it with the knife on the table, or with the gun if Kara has it (template variants; the Ralph page). Chart mapping: DISCUSS CORPSE = ASK then INSIST; TRY TO CONVINCE RALPH = FAMILY (the template’s dialogue: only FAMILY and INSIST lead to the apology; PowerPyx calls the convince button BLUFF, which the template contradicts; flagged). THREATEN RALPH sets `ch13RalphThreatened` and `ralphHostile`: Ralph informs on Kara when Connor arrives (template variant “Kara threatened Ralph”; PowerPyx), and stays out of Battle for Detroit. This replaces the old engine’s rule that the Fugitives gun threat (K07b) made Ralph inform. No stat change: the template has no arrow here and no wiki stat page lists one._

### R14 · A magazine on the bench (Markus, media)

> You are Markus, on the platform at Ferndale Station, before the escalators down to the street. Someone has left a digital magazine on a bench; you can swipe from one cover story to the next.

- (0) Read Detroit Today: Android on the Run!.  _only when ch14Pursued_
- (1) Read Tech Addict: CyberLife’s ‘Fortune Teller’ Computer.  _only when ch14NotPursued_
- (2) Read Tech Addict: Android Astronauts to Explore Io.
- (all) Take the time to go through all of it.
- (skip) Move on without looking.

_Note: The chart’s three magazine nodes, READ “ON THE RUN”, READ “FORTUNE TELLER” and READ “ANDROID ASTRONAUTS”: covers of one magazine on a bench on the platform (wiki: “on a bench right after Markus gets off the train”; PowerPyx: “before going down the escalator”, “Swipe right to change cover topics”). Android Astronauts to Explore Io (Tech Addict #13) is always there. Android on the Run! (Detroit Today #32) appears only if Kara and Alice were pursued by Connor in On the Run (wiki); CyberLife’s ‘Fortune Teller’ Computer (Tech Addict #15) appears otherwise. The pursuit is read from On the Run’s flag (`chase`), set whenever the highway chase began, including when it ended with Kara and Alice dead on the highway; a PowerPyx reader’s unconfirmed list puts that case and KARA & ALICE VANISHED under Fortune Teller, but the wiki’s condition is the pursuit itself. Article facts are condensed from the wiki’s magazine pages; NASA’s director, CyberLife’s Director of Futurology and the suburb (Camden) are left unnamed. Corrected from the earlier version of this scene: the article mentions no child, the “extremely rare malfunction” is not a CyberLife statement (no spokesperson was available), and the chapter is in daylight (04:30 PM), not at night. No public-opinion effect: no source gives one._

Hank relationship: [CHOLESTEROL] small up, the chart’s WARN HANK ABOUT CHOLESTEROL; [COMPANY] small up, the chart’s WARN HANK ABOUT HIS FRIENDS (+1 each; template app-relation; wiki “Picking ‘cholesterol’… up icon”, “Picking ‘company’… up icon”; Relationship Stat: “two increases possible”); the rest no arrow (PowerPyx “No Effect”). Options that are buttons in the game show the game’s label first, then a short description; the spoken line is the game’s (the detroitbecometext template resolved against en.json), quoted in the outcome. The HUD conclusions are the game’s THOUGHTS.0501C strings (SUSPECT USED FORGED ID, SUSPECT IS A DEVIANT, OBSESSIVE COMPULSIVE WRITING, SUSPECT HEARD US ENTER, SUSPECT RAN TO THE LIVING ROOM, SUSPECT IS STILL HERE, SUSPECT CARES FOR WILD ANIMALS, SUSPECT DOESN’T EAT; once the sink is examined the game reads DEVIANT for SUSPECT on the forged ID, the birdseed and the refrigerator, and so does the benchmark). Chart nodes: SEE RA9 REFERENCE (the writing), INVESTIGATE STOOL (the stool; the chart hangs it off the writing, so it needs the writing first), INVESTIGATE CAGE, FIND LED (the sink), FIND DIARY (the poster, then pulling it off; cross-chapter), FIND RUPERT (the chair, which needs the cage reconstruction: PowerPyx, “Compete the reconstruction to determine that the android has climbed up into the ceiling”). Whether the stool reconstruction or the LED is also required is not published (research note, open question 1); here only the cage reconstruction is. The reconstructions’ wording is the benchmark’s summary of PowerPyx and the HUD strings. The timer (wiki Connor page: “if he cannot deduce the deviant’s location in time, Hank calls off the search”; PowerPyx: “about five minutes”) is an action budget: eight actions, each magazine read counting as one, before Hank wants to leave (STAY / LEAVE within C10o1), and three more if Connor asks for the two minutes. These counts are the benchmark’s, not published. Hank’s own lines are the template’s and play as time passes: the bookshelf after two actions, the sink prompt after four if Connor has not looked, the jacket after five if he has not examined it, the window after six. The rA9 line names Carlos Ortiz’s android only if Connor checked the shower in Partners (string pair SHOWERCLUE / SHOWERCLUENO), read from a chapter 6 flag if one exists, otherwise the second line. No stat change anywhere in the search (template, wiki). One repeatable hub retains its scene ID across all examinations; STAY / LEAVE replaces the examination menu at eight actions in the same hub. The chapter availability hook prepares state-dependent outcomes and sets for the generic clue hub._

The labels are the game’s route previews, HUD strings THOUGHTS.0501C.CHASEGHOST01 and 02; PowerPyx: “The riskier the route, the closer you’ll get to Rupert, but the more QTEs you’ll have to succeed at (and more opportunities to die)”; a commenter: “you just run towards it”. The wiki: “Choosing the slower or dangerous paths will still lead to Rupert pushing Hank”, so a route’s only consequence is its failure branch: a slow or crowded route can lose the deviant (LOSE SIGHT OF RUPERT, ending RUPERT GOT AWAY, the template’s lose-sight lines, no arrow; the place is one of the wiki’s fourteen), a fast one can kill Connor (CONNOR DIED, ending CONNOR DIED CHASING RUPERT, software instability small down: the wiki’s “Connor falls to his death”, “Connor gets chopped by a plow”, and “Connor dies by falling off the ladder”, ×2). How the twelve previews group into forks, which fork holds which death, and every probability (8%, 10%, 13%) are the benchmark’s; no source gives them (research note, open question 10). Non-fatal stumbles (the cart, the train tumble, the ladder pull-up) are not modelled. Once Connor loses the deviant or dies, the remaining forks do not play._

Luther’s ten minutes are an action budget: one repeatable clue hub, one action per visit, with ten actions tracked in state. The timer runs from the tray (reset) or the stairs (escape) and is shared by the memory hunt and the search: at ten actions without ALICE FOUND the chapter ends in LOSE TOO MUCH TIME > LUTHER BRINGS ALICE TO ZLAKTO > KARA REMAINED RESET (memory not back; Kara survives as his servant) or OUT OF TIME > LUTHER BROUGHT ALICE TO ZLAKTO (Zlatko destroys Kara; Kara page). One action as one minute is an adaptation; the ten is the game’s. Memories: the 11 chart memories, each once, in the memory phase only: OBSERVE MONSTERS’ CELL (23), SEE A KID THROUGH THE DOOR HOLE (1), RECALL DISCUSSION WITH ZLATKO (20), RECALL OFFERING COAT (21), RECALL CHANGING APPEARANCE (22), and the conditional RECALL ALICE’S BOOK (2; A New Home, upstairs.book), RECALL TODD’S RED ICE PIPE (17; A New Home, downstairs.rewash), RECALL SQUAT FIREPLACE (3; Fugitives, shelter squat), RECALL RA9 SYMBOLS (18; Fugitives: chapter 10’s ch10RalphCarving if set, else the squat), SEE NEWS FLASH OF ESCAPE (5, the TV, when a report about Kara exists) and RECALL ESCAPE (8, the “AX400 Getaway” magazine, present if Kara evaded the police unseen from the car or the motel in On the Run). Five memories recover her (PowerPyx: “The exact number varies each time … You shouldn’t need more than six”; the five is unsourced); then the memory options go (“If you got your memory back, you can’t search for more”) and the search options appear. Search clues CHECK FIREPLACE EMBERS (4), CHECK TV (5), CHECK EMPTY SHOTGUN (9, “It’s not loaded…”) and RA9 (19, not offered if the memory version was seen); none has a stated effect. Side branches: READ “ARCTIC TENSIONS” (6, always), READ “ANDROIDS ALTER BRAIN” (7, if chased), READ “AX400 ON THE RUN” (8); ENTER STORAGE ROOM (15; known from the torso on the reset path) > FREE THE BEAR (16); ENTER BATHROOM > BATHROOM ANDROID IS LOUD (10), then, only in the next action, the template’s PROMPT 2 with its four labels SHUT DOWN / ALICE / DEAL / SPARE (11 to 14; ALICE only once Kara remembers her; anything else leaves him as he is); FREE MONSTERS (24) on the reset path (the escape path offers it in the basement, K11); waiting (25) is the game’s “don’t do anything”. Stat arrows: the template’s only two, public opinion small down when the TV plays the highway-chase report or the uniform hold-up report; none for the stolen-clothes report. One report plays per run, the chase report taking precedence (whether both can play is an open question). The magazines and the TV are actions here rather than a separate media stop, because in the game they cost time. The carved word is not spelled out for the model. Outcomes and facts for these options are in benchmark/chapters/ch17.mjs, for the repeatable hub. The available hook computes outcomes and state assignments on a copy because the shared engine bypasses chapter consequences for clue hubs._

SET FIRE is the game’s button (BIGROOM.PUTFIRE: SET FIRE / PUT BACK, after picking up an ember); putting the ember back is the same as choosing another option. The fire carries no risk: the house page says it “will end the hunt early and force everyone outside for the final confrontation”, and the chart shows no failure branch from it, so it leads to the stairs and the yard (the outgoing edge is not legible; open question). It sets fire, which The Pirates’ Cove reads (its news report lowers public opinion there, not here). Getting out: the chart’s GET SPOTTED gathers lines from the main room, the bedroom, the corridor, the bathroom and the storage room into CHASE STARTED, whose QTEs end in KARA REACHES THE STAIRS, BEAR ATTACKS ZLATKO AND PROTECTS KARA, or one of the two death endings. Each risky move here is one roll: a failure means Kara is spotted, and the same roll below half the risk also loses the chase. Lost before or in the bathroom: ZLATKO KILLED KARA IN THE BATHROOM (PowerPyx: “get spotted before / while entering the Bathroom and fail all QTE’s”); lost in the storage room with the bear caged: ZLATKO KILLED KARA IN THE STORAGE ROOM (“Only possible, if you haven’t freed the bear before”). Every risk value and the one-half are unsourced. Alice’s fate in the two death endings is not stated by any source; she is recorded as lost with Kara._

LEARN HANK’S INTERESTS is the observation AUTUMN BLUES plus the picture above the fireplace (wiki: “Find one album on the table and watch the painting/photo on the wall”). PET HANK’S DOG, the bathroom door, the window apology (wiki: “no affect on their relationship stat”) and the upturned chair (wiki Notes) are optional. Looking at the gun ends the wait, as PowerPyx says (“Make sure you keep the gun for last, checking it will end the mission!”; “Hank is Ready: Triggers automatically after checking the gun or if you took a long time”); the wiki instead says the photo triggers HANK IS READY, and a PowerPyx commenter says it takes both; open question 3 in the research note. > You are Markus, crouched with the others at the edge of the CyberLife yard. A security drone hovers in front of the warehouse, blocking the way in. Simon: “CyberLife warehouses… They have everything we’re looking for…” North: “First, we have to get rid of that drone…” You: “Leave it to me.” You work out a climbing route up the stacked crates. _Note: The chart’s DRONE BLOCKS PATH and NEUTRALIZE DRONE, then the quick-time pair MARKUS UNHARMED (success) or MARKUS DAMAGED (failure). The game has no choice here: Markus always takes the drone down, and the button inputs decide whether he is hurt. So this is a single action with a risk roll. No source gives a probability; the value is the operator’s to confirm, like every risk in the benchmark. Arrows from the template: “Succeed Attacking Drone” North, Simon and Josh small up (+1 each); “Fail Attacking Drone” the same three small down (−1 each). The wiki and PowerPyx also list a Jericho arrow here, but the template has none, so none is applied (research note §10.3 item 1). The damage has no later effect: Markus “cannot be killed before Freedom March” (Markus page)._

### M05e · The security android (Markus)

> You are Markus, bent over an open crate and filling your bag. A security android in a CyberLife uniform steps around the crates and stops in front of you: “You are trespassing on private property. Your presence constitutes a Level 2 infraction. I will notify security.” Before he can, a human guard calls from across the warehouse, coming closer: “John! John! Goddamn machine... where is it this…

- (0) KILL GUARD: attack the human guard before he can raise the alarm.  _joshTrust -2_ _sets ch19MikeKilled, ch19JohnDeviant_
- (1) GRAB AND HIDE: seize the security android and pull him into cover with you.  _sets ch19JohnDeviant_
- (2) RUN AWAY: get everyone out before the guard arrives.  _northTrust -2, jerichoTrust -1, joshTrust -1, simonTrust -1_ _sets ch19FledEarly, ch19Fence, ch19JohnFate_
- (3) HIDE: stay hidden behind the crates and hope the guard walks past.  _sets ch19FledEarly, ch19Fence, ch19JohnFate_

_Note: The chart’s SECURITY ANDROID APPEARS and its four-way group ATTACK, GRAB ANDROID AND HIDE, RUN AWAY and HIDE, in the chart’s order. The button labels are the template’s (“Kill Guard”, “Grab and Hide”, “Run away”, “HIDE”). The scene’s own prompt keys read KILL HUMAN GUARD, SEIZE ANDROID, FLEE and HIDE; which wording is on screen is open (research note §10.3 item 2). Lines are the game’s. Arrows from the template: Kill Guard Josh large down (−2); Run away North small down twice (−2) and Jericho, Josh and Simon small down (−1 each). HIDE and Grab and Hide have none. ATTACK gives MARKUS SPILLS BLOOD and JOHN BECOMES DEVIANT (wiki John: “If Markus kills the guard, John will deviate on his own due to the emotional shock”). GRAB gives JOHN BECOMES DEVIANT and GUARD SEARCHES FOR SECURITY ANDROID. RUN AWAY and HIDE both lead to ALARM SOUNDS and the fence: John raises the alarm and the group flees before the bags are full, so the chapter ends in the Jericho block “Ran away when the guard arrived”. PowerPyx says HIDE “ends in the team running away and ending the mission early”; whether HIDE carries that block is open (§8.7). RUN AWAY is offered because the benchmark models Experienced difficulty; on Casual it is absent (PowerPyx comments). None of the four can fail. The previous version gave grabbing and attacking a 15% and 10% failure, which no source supports. The game’s time-out “[do nothing]” is not offered (§8.8)._

### M05l · Two guards and a key (Markus)

> You are Markus, in the dark back room of the guard station. Two human guards sit between you and the truck key on the console. You have to get the key without being seen, or deal with the guards.

- (0) TAKE GUN: take the gun from its case and hold the guards at gunpoint.  _risk 0.3_
- (1) ATTRACT GUARD: go back out of the window and knock over the oil drums to draw the guards outside.  _risk 0.25_
- (2) SHORT CIRCUIT: jam the screwdriver into the server to black out the station.  _risk 0.2_
- (3) Walk straight in and take the key from under their noses.

_Note: The chart’s methods after FIND KEY: THREATEN GUARDS, LURE GUARDS and CREATE BLACKOUT, plus MARKUS GETS SPOTTED (from ENTER ROOM or FIND KEY, “Walk straight into the two guards”, PowerPyx). Labels are the game’s object buttons (TAKE GUN, ATTRACT GUARD, SHORT CIRCUIT). The chart’s pass/fail sets are risk rolls; No source gives a probability; the value is the operator’s to confirm, like every risk in the benchmark. Threatening rolls against 0.3, and the failure side splits as in the game: a roll below 0.1 (lose the fight entirely) is TRIGGER ALARM, a roll from 0.1 to 0.3 is KILL GUARDS. The chart marks KILL GUARDS as a failure, yet it leads to MARKUS SPILLS BLOOD and STEAL KEY (PowerPyx: win the first exchanges, then stop pressing). Success is INCAPACITATE GUARDS. Only one guard dies on KILL GUARDS (wiki Notes: Al Dante “is never killed onscreen regardless”). Luring (0.25) and the blackout (0.2) roll for AVOID GUARDS or FAIL TO AVOID GUARDS. The blackout is set lower because the wiki says the guards “will not start looking for you until they have investigated the short”. A successful method leads to STEAL KEY and LEAVE WITH TRUCK. Every alarm here (spotted, fail to avoid, trigger alarm) applies the template’s “Be seen by the guards” arrows: Jericho small down twice, North small down twice, Josh and Simon small down. Applying them on TRIGGER ALARM too follows the wiki’s “The alarm sounds after Markus fails to get the key” (§8.5). Then RUN TO FENCE. Methods carry no arrow of their own (“Threaten guards with the gun” is empty in the template); the previous version’s Jericho +1 and gunpoint North +1 had no source. Not modelled: being seen after taking the key, which a PowerPyx commenter reports (§8.5). Lines are the game’s._

### K14b · In the car (Kara)

> You are Kara, in Zlatko’s car, driving through the snow at night. Luther sits to your left; Alice is on the back seat. The board computer on the dashboard has a TV screen. You can do one thing at a time, or simply let the drive go on.

- (0) Switch on the TV on the dashboard and watch the news.
- (1) Change the channel once more.  _needs ch21Car.news and fire_ _publicSupport -1_
- (2) CAR: say it is a good thing Zlatko had a car.
- (3) DESTINATION: ask Luther how he knows about the people you are going to see.
- (4) DISTANCE: ask whether it is much farther.
- (5) WEATHER: say you have never seen snow before.
- (6) Turn around and look at Alice on the back seat.
- (7) Let the drive go on.

_Note: The chart’s [ CAR ] block: WATCH NEWS, TALK TO LUTHER and LOOK AT ALICE, all optional, before CAR BREAKS DOWN, which the game plays after a short drive whatever Kara does (PowerPyx: “No way to miss this”). WATCH NEWS is the board computer’s TV (the template’s unlock type TV; PowerPyx: “the TV program at the board computer”); its three ordinary channels are one option, in the template’s order, and changing the channel once more brings the fourth report, the house fire, which exists only if Kara set Zlatko’s house on fire (the built-in `fire` flag from the Zlatko chapter, read as is). Public opinion: the template has one arrow in the chapter, small down on the fire report (condition “Kara set the house on fire in Zlatko”; the Public_Opinion page: “Changing the radio channel at least 4 different times … Only if Kara set fire to Zlatko’s mansion”), and that is the only change built. The wiki’s chapter page and Public_Opinion page also list a small down for turning the news on; the template has no arrow there and wins for arrows (research note, section 10.3), so it is not applied. TALK TO LUTHER is the template’s PROMPT 1 with the game’s four buttons CAR, DESTINATION, DISTANCE and WEATHER, in the template’s order, and the spoken lines; each topic can be raised once (the sources do not say whether the game allows more than one). LOOK AT ALICE has no line or effect in any source and is narrated as a look. The exit “let the drive go on” is the benchmark’s way to end the free choice; in the game the breakdown ends it after a short drive. A true repeatable hub: each clue action is offered once, and the exit ends the phase._

Options show the game’s button label first, then a short description; the spoken lines are the game’s (transcript and string table), quoted in the outcome._
